    25→class TestStripTsComments:
    26→    def test_strips_line_comment(self):
    27→        assert _strip_ts_comments("code // comment") == "code "
    28→
    29→    def test_strips_block_comment(self):
    30→        assert _strip_ts_comments("before /* block */ after") == "before  after"
    31→
    32→    def test_preserves_string_with_slashes(self):
    33→        result = _strip_ts_comments('const url = "http://example.com";')
    34→        assert "http://example.com" in result
    35→
    36→    def test_preserves_single_quoted_string(self):
    37→        result = _strip_ts_comments("const s = '// not a comment';")
    38→        assert "// not a comment" in result
    39→
    40→    def test_preserves_template_literal(self):
    41→        result = _strip_ts_comments("const s = `// not a comment`;")
    42→        assert "// not a comment" in result
    43→
    44→    def test_strips_multiline_block_comment(self):
    45→        text = "start /* this is\na multiline\ncomment */ end"
    46→        result = _strip_ts_comments(text)
    47→        assert "start" in result
    48→        assert "end" in result
    49→        assert "multiline" not in result
    50→
    51→    def test_empty_input(self):
    52→        assert _strip_ts_comments("") == ""
    53→
    54→    def test_no_comments(self):
    55→        code = "const x = 1;\nconst y = 2;"
    56→        assert _strip_ts_comments(code) == code
    57→
    58→    def test_nested_string_in_comment_region(self):
    59→        # The comment contains string-like characters
    60→        result = _strip_ts_comments('code /* "not a string" */ more')
    61→        assert "code" in result
    62→        assert "more" in result
    63→        assert "not a string" not in result
    64→
    65→    def test_unterminated_block_comment(self):
    66→        # Unterminated block comment -- should strip to end
    67→        result = _strip_ts_comments("code /* unterminated")
    68→        assert result == "code "
    69→
    70→    def test_unterminated_line_comment(self):
    71→        result = _strip_ts_comments("code // line comment no newline")
    72→        assert result == "code "

    78→class TestTsMatchIsInString:
    79→    def test_match_in_double_quoted_string(self):
    80→        line = 'const s = "any type";'
    81→        pos = line.index("any")
    82→        assert _ts_match_is_in_string(line, pos) is True
    83→
    84→    def test_match_in_single_quoted_string(self):
    85→        line = "const s = 'any type';"
    86→        pos = line.index("any")
    87→        assert _ts_match_is_in_string(line, pos) is True
    88→
    89→    def test_match_in_template_literal(self):
    90→        line = "const s = `any type`;"
    91→        pos = line.index("any")
    92→        assert _ts_match_is_in_string(line, pos) is True
    93→
    94→    def test_match_in_code(self):
    95→        line = "const x: any = 5;"
    96→        pos = line.index("any")
    97→        assert _ts_match_is_in_string(line, pos) is False
    98→
    99→    def test_match_in_line_comment(self):
   100→        line = "const x = 1; // any type here"
   101→        pos = line.index("any")
   102→        assert _ts_match_is_in_string(line, pos) is True
   103→
   104→    def test_match_after_escaped_quote(self):
   105→        line = r"const s = 'it\'s any type';"
   106→        # After the escaped quote, "any" is still inside the string
   107→        pos = line.index("any")
   108→        assert _ts_match_is_in_string(line, pos) is True
   109→
   110→    def test_match_at_start_of_line(self):
   111→        line = "any = 5;"
   112→        assert _ts_match_is_in_string(line, 0) is False
   113→
   114→    def test_match_after_string_closes(self):
   115→        line = "const s = 'hi'; const x: any = 5;"
   116→        pos = line.rindex("any")
   117→        assert _ts_match_is_in_string(line, pos) is False
   118→
   119→    def test_empty_line(self):
   120→        assert _ts_match_is_in_string("", 0) is False

   197→def _make_counts():

   202→class TestDetectAsyncNoAwait:
   203→    def test_flags_async_without_await(self):
   204→        content = "async function fetchData() {\n  return 1;\n}\n"
   205→        lines = content.splitlines()
   206→        counts = _make_counts()
   207→        _detect_async_no_await("test.ts", content, lines, counts)
   208→        assert len(counts["async_no_await"]) == 1
   209→
   210→    def test_skips_async_with_await(self):
   211→        content = "async function fetchData() {\n  const d = await fetch('/');\n  return d;\n}\n"
   212→        lines = content.splitlines()
   213→        counts = _make_counts()
   214→        _detect_async_no_await("test.ts", content, lines, counts)
   215→        assert len(counts["async_no_await"]) == 0
   216→
   217→    def test_arrow_async_without_await(self):
   218→        content = "const fn = async () => {\n  return 42;\n}\n"
   219→        lines = content.splitlines()
   220→        counts = _make_counts()
   221→        _detect_async_no_await("test.ts", content, lines, counts)
   222→        assert len(counts["async_no_await"]) == 1
   223→
   224→    def test_await_in_comment_still_flagged(self):
   225→        """'await' inside a comment should not count — function is still flagged."""
   226→        content = "async function fetchData() {\n  // await fetch('/');\n  return 1;\n}\n"
   227→        lines = content.splitlines()
   228→        counts = _make_counts()
   229→        _detect_async_no_await("test.ts", content, lines, counts)
   230→        assert len(counts["async_no_await"]) == 1
   231→
   232→    def test_await_in_string_still_flagged(self):
   233→        """'await' inside a string literal should not count — function is still flagged."""
   234→        content = "async function fetchData() {\n  const s = 'await something';\n  return s;\n}\n"
   235→        lines = content.splitlines()
   236→        counts = _make_counts()
   237→        _detect_async_no_await("test.ts", content, lines, counts)
   238→        assert len(counts["async_no_await"]) == 1
   239→
   240→    def test_await_beyond_line_200_detected(self):
   241→        """Await past old 200-line cap should still be detected (limit is now 2000)."""
   242→        filler = "\n".join(f"  const x{i} = {i};" for i in range(250))
   243→        content = f"async function big() {{\n{filler}\n  const d = await fetch('/');\n  return d;\n}}\n"
   244→        lines = content.splitlines()
   245→        counts = _make_counts()
   246→        _detect_async_no_await("test.ts", content, lines, counts)
   247→        assert len(counts["async_no_await"]) == 0

   383→class TestDetectSwallowedErrors:

   428→    """Declaration files (.d.ts) are skipped."""
   429→
   430→    _write(tmp_path, "types.d.ts", "declare const x: any;\n")
   431→    entries, _ = detect_smells(tmp_path)
   432→    any_entries = [e for e in entries if e["id"] == "any_type"]
   433→    assert len(any_entries) == 0
   434→
   435→
   436→def test_smells_in_block_comments_not_flagged(tmp_path):
   437→    """Code patterns inside block comments should not be detected."""
   438→
   439→    _write(
   440→        tmp_path,
   441→        "ok.ts",
   442→        (
   443→            "/* This is a block comment\n"
   444→            "   const x: any = 1;\n"
   445→            "*/\n"
   446→            "const y: string = 'clean';\n"
   447→        ),
   448→    )
   449→    entries, _ = detect_smells(tmp_path)
   450→    any_entries = [e for e in entries if e["id"] == "any_type"]
   451→    assert len(any_entries) == 0
   452→
   453→
   454→def test_smells_in_string_literals_not_flagged(tmp_path):
   455→    """Patterns inside string literals should not be detected."""
   456→
   457→    _write(tmp_path, "ok.ts", "const msg = 'This is: any type of message';\n")
   458→    entries, _ = detect_smells(tmp_path)
   459→    any_entries = [e for e in entries if e["id"] == "any_type"]
   460→    assert len(any_entries) == 0
   461→
   462→
   463→def test_clean_file_no_smells(tmp_path):
   464→    """A clean file should produce no smell entries."""
   465→
   466→    _write(
   467→        tmp_path,
   468→        "clean.ts",
   469→        (
   470→            "const x: string = 'hello';\n"
   471→            "const y: number = 42;\n"
   472→            "function greet(name: string): string {\n"
   473→            "  return `Hello, ${name}!`;\n"
   474→            "}\n"
   475→        ),
   476→    )
   477→    entries, total = detect_smells(tmp_path)
   478→    assert entries == []
   479→    assert total == 1
   480→
   481→
   482→def test_returns_file_count(tmp_path):
   483→    """detect_smells returns the total number of files checked."""
   484→
   485→    _write(tmp_path, "a.ts", "const a = 1;\n")
   486→    _write(tmp_path, "b.tsx", "const b = 2;\n")
   487→    _, total = detect_smells(tmp_path)
   488→    assert total == 2
   489→
   490→
   491→def test_entries_sorted_by_severity_then_count(tmp_path):
   492→    """Entries should be sorted by severity (high first) then by descending count."""
   493→
   494→    _write(
   495→        tmp_path,
   496→        "bad.ts",
   497→        (
   498→            "try { foo(); } catch (e) { }\n"  # empty_catch (high)
   499→            "const x: any = 1;\n"  # any_type (medium)
   500→            "const y: any = 2;\n"  # any_type (medium) second hit
   501→        ),
   502→    )
   503→    entries, _ = detect_smells(tmp_path)
   504→    if len(entries) >= 2:
   505→        severity_order = {"high": 0, "medium": 1, "low": 2}
   506→        for i in range(len(entries) - 1):
   507→            cur_sev = severity_order.get(entries[i]["severity"], 9)
   508→            next_sev = severity_order.get(entries[i + 1]["severity"], 9)
   509→            if cur_sev == next_sev:
   510→                assert entries[i]["count"] >= entries[i + 1]["count"]
   511→            else:
   512→                assert cur_sev <= next_sev
   513→

1→"""Multi-line smell detection helpers (brace-tracked).
     2→
     3→Shared utilities (string-aware scanning, brace tracking, comment stripping)
     4→plus a handful of smell detectors. Monster-function, dead-function,
     5→window-global, catch-return-default, and switch-no-default detectors live
     6→in _smell_detectors.py.
     7→"""
     8→
     9→from __future__ import annotations
    10→
    11→import re
    12→
    13→from desloppify.core.text_api import strip_c_style_comments
    14→from desloppify.languages.typescript.syntax.scanner import scan_code
    15→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_effects import (
    16→    detect_dead_useeffects as _detect_dead_useeffects_impl,
    17→)
    18→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_effects import (
    19→    detect_empty_if_chains as _detect_empty_if_chains_impl,
    20→)
    21→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_effects import (
    22→    detect_error_no_throw as _detect_error_no_throw_impl,
    23→)
    24→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_effects import (
    25→    detect_swallowed_errors as _detect_swallowed_errors_impl,
    26→)
    27→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_effects import (
    28→    track_brace_body as _track_brace_body_impl,
    29→)
    30→
    31→
    32→def _strip_ts_comments(text: str) -> str:
    33→    """Strip // and /* */ comments while preserving strings.
    34→
    35→    Delegates to the shared implementation in utils.py.
    36→    """
    37→    return strip_c_style_comments(text)
    38→
    39→
    40→def _ts_match_is_in_string(line: str, match_start: int) -> bool:
    41→    """Check if a match position falls inside a string literal or comment on a single line.
    42→
    43→    Mirrors Python's _match_is_in_string but for TS syntax (', ", `, //).
    44→    """
    45→    i = 0
    46→    in_str = None
    47→
    48→    while i < len(line):
    49→        if i == match_start:
    50→            return in_str is not None
    51→
    52→        ch = line[i]
    53→
    54→        # Escape sequences inside strings
    55→        if in_str and ch == "\\" and i + 1 < len(line):
    56→            i += 2
    57→            continue
    58→
    59→        if in_str:
    60→            if ch == in_str:
    61→                in_str = None
    62→            i += 1
    63→            continue
    64→
    65→        # Line comment — everything after is non-code
    66→        if ch == "/" and i + 1 < len(line) and line[i + 1] == "/":
    67→            return match_start > i
    68→
    69→        if ch in ("'", '"', "`"):
    70→            in_str = ch
    71→            i += 1
    72→            continue
    73→
    74→        i += 1
    75→
    76→    return False
    77→
    78→
    79→def _detect_async_no_await(
    80→    filepath: str, content: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
    81→):
    82→    """Find async functions that don't use await.
    83→
    84→    Algorithm: for each async declaration, track brace depth to find the function
    85→    body extent (up to 2000 lines). For each line, accumulate code-only characters
    86→    (skipping strings/comments via scan_code) and regex for \\bawait\\b on the
    87→    code-only text. If the opening brace closes without seeing await, flag it.
    88→    """
    89→    async_re = re.compile(r"(?:async\s+function\s+(\w+)|(\w+)\s*=\s*async)")
    90→    await_re = re.compile(r"\bawait\b")
    91→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
    92→        m = async_re.search(line)
    93→        if not m:
    94→            continue
    95→        name = m.group(1) or m.group(2)
    96→        brace_depth = 0
    97→        found_open = False
    98→        has_await = False
    99→        for j in range(i, min(i + 2000, len(lines))):
   100→            body_line = lines[j]
   101→            # Build code-only text for this line (no strings, no comments)
   102→            code_chars: list[str] = []
   103→            prev_code_ch = ""
   104→            for _, ch, in_s in scan_code(body_line):
   105→                if in_s:
   106→                    continue
   107→                if ch == "/" and prev_code_ch == "/":
   108→                    break  # Rest of line is comment
   109→                elif ch == "{":
   110→                    brace_depth += 1
   111→                    found_open = True
   112→                elif ch == "}":
   113→                    brace_depth -= 1
   114→                code_chars.append(ch)
   115→                prev_code_ch = ch
   116→            if not has_await and await_re.search("".join(code_chars)):
   117→                has_await = True
   118→            if found_open and brace_depth <= 0:
   119→                break
   120→
   121→        if found_open and not has_await:
   122→            smell_counts["async_no_await"].append(
   123→                {
   124→                    "file": filepath,
   125→                    "line": i + 1,
   126→                    "content": f"async {name or '(anonymous)'} has no await",
   127→                }
   128→            )
   129→
   130→
   131→def _detect_error_no_throw(
   132→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   133→):
   134→    """Find console.error calls not followed by throw or return."""
   135→    _detect_error_no_throw_impl(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   136→
   137→
   138→def _detect_empty_if_chains(
   139→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   140→):
   141→    """Find if/else chains where all branches are empty."""
   142→    _detect_empty_if_chains_impl(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   143→
   144→
   145→def _detect_dead_useeffects(
   146→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   147→):
   148→    """Find useEffect calls with empty or whitespace/comment-only bodies.
   149→
   150→    Algorithm: two-pass brace/paren tracking with string-escape awareness.
   151→    Pass 1: track paren depth to find the full useEffect(...) extent.
   152→    Pass 2: within that extent, find the arrow body ({...} after =>) using
   153→    brace depth, skipping characters inside string literals (', ", `).
   154→    Then strip comments from the body and check if anything remains.
   155→    """
   156→    _detect_dead_useeffects_impl(
   157→        filepath,
   158→        lines,
   159→        smell_counts,
   160→        scan_code_fn=scan_code,
   161→        strip_ts_comments_fn=_strip_ts_comments,
   162→    )
   163→
   164→
   165→def _detect_swallowed_errors(
   166→    filepath: str, content: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   167→):
   168→    """Find catch blocks whose only content is console.error/warn/log (swallowed errors).
   169→
   170→    Algorithm: regex-find each `catch(...) {`, then track brace depth with
   171→    string-escape awareness to extract the catch body (up to 500 chars).
   172→    Strip comments, split into statements, and check if every statement
   173→    is a console.error/warn/log call.
   174→    """
   175→    _detect_swallowed_errors_impl(
   176→        filepath,
   177→        content,
   178→        lines,
   179→        smell_counts,
   180→        scan_code_fn=scan_code,
   181→        strip_ts_comments_fn=_strip_ts_comments,
   182→    )
   183→
   184→
   185→def _track_brace_body(
   186→    lines: list[str], start_line: int, *, max_scan: int = 2000
   187→) -> int | None:
   188→    """Find the closing brace that matches the first opening brace from start_line.
   189→
   190→    Tracks brace depth with string-literal awareness (', ", `).
   191→    Returns the line index of the closing brace, or None if not found.
   192→    """
   193→    return _track_brace_body_impl(
   194→        lines,
   195→        start_line,
   196→        scan_code_fn=scan_code,
   197→        max_scan=max_scan,
   198→    )
   199→

tool_result Read
```
1→"""Extended TypeScript smell detection routines."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import os
     6→import re
     7→
     8→# Basenames that indicate logger/error-handler utility files (case-insensitive).
     9→_ERROR_HANDLER_BASENAMES = ("logger", "errorpresentation", "errorhandler", "errorreporting")
    10→
    11→# Patterns in preceding context that indicate legitimate error handling.
    12→_PRECEDING_SKIP_PATTERNS = re.compile(
    13→    r"componentDidCatch|import\.meta\.env\.DEV|process\.env\.NODE_ENV"
    14→)
    15→
    16→# Patterns in following lines that count as "handled" (not just throw/return).
    17→_HANDLED_RE = re.compile(
    18→    r"\b(?:throw|return)\b|toast\(|normalizeAndPresentError\(|presentError\(|rethrow"
    19→)
    20→
    21→
    22→def detect_error_no_throw(
    23→    filepath: str,
    24→    lines: list[str],
    25→    smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]],
    26→) -> None:
    27→    """Find console.error calls not followed by throw/return or other handling."""
    28→    # Skip files that are themselves logger/error-handler utilities.
    29→    basename = os.path.basename(filepath).lower()
    30→    basename_no_ext = os.path.splitext(basename)[0]
    31→    if any(tag in basename_no_ext for tag in _ERROR_HANDLER_BASENAMES):
    32→        return
    33→
    34→    for index, line in enumerate(lines):
    35→        if "console.error" not in line:
    36→            continue
    37→
    38→        # Skip if preceding 10 lines contain error-boundary or dev-only context.
    39→        preceding = "\n".join(lines[max(0, index - 10) : index])
    40→        if _PRECEDING_SKIP_PATTERNS.search(preceding):
    41→            continue
    42→
    43→        following = "\n".join(lines[index + 1 : index + 4])
    44→        if not _HANDLED_RE.search(following):
    45→            smell_counts["console_error_no_throw"].append(
    46→                {
    47→                    "file": filepath,
    48→                    "line": index + 1,
    49→                    "content": line.strip()[:100],
    50→                }
    51→            )
    52→
    53→
    54→def detect_empty_if_chains(
    55→    filepath: str,
    56→    lines: list[str],
    57→    smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]],
    58→) -> None:
    59→    """Find if/else chains where all branches are empty."""
    60→    index = 0
    61→    while index < len(lines):
    62→        stripped = lines[index].strip()
    63→        if not re.match(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\(", stripped):
    64→            index += 1
    65→            continue
    66→
    67→        if re.match(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*\}\s*$", stripped):
    68→            chain_start = index
    69→            cursor = index + 1
    70→            while cursor < len(lines):
    71→                next_stripped = lines[cursor].strip()
    72→                if re.match(r"else\s+if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*\}\s*$", next_stripped):
    73→                    cursor += 1
    74→                    continue
    75→                if re.match(r"(?:\}\s*)?else\s*\{\s*\}\s*$", next_stripped):
    76→                    cursor += 1
    77→                    continue
    78→                break
    79→            smell_counts["empty_if_chain"].append(
    80→                {
    81→                    "file": filepath,
    82→                    "line": chain_start + 1,
    83→                    "content": stripped[:100],
    84→                }
    85→            )
    86→            index = cursor
    87→            continue
    88→
    89→        if re.match(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*$", stripped):
    90→            chain_start = index
    91→            chain_all_empty = True
    92→            cursor = index
    93→            while cursor < len(lines):
    94→                current = lines[cursor].strip()
    95→                if cursor == chain_start:
    96→                    if not re.match(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*$", current):
    97→                        chain_all_empty = False
    98→                        break
    99→                elif re.match(r"\}\s*else\s+if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*$", current):
   100→                    pass
   101→                elif re.match(r"\}\s*else\s*\{\s*$", current):
   102→                    pass
   103→                elif current == "}":
   104→                    lookahead = cursor + 1
   105→                    while lookahead < len(lines) and lines[lookahead].strip() == "":
   106→                        lookahead += 1
   107→                    if lookahead < len(lines) and re.match(
   108→                        r"else\s", lines[lookahead].strip()
   109→                    ):
   110→                        cursor = lookahead
   111→                        continue
   112→                    cursor += 1
   113→                    break
   114→                elif current == "":
   115→                    cursor += 1
   116→                    continue
   117→                else:
   118→                    chain_all_empty = False
   119→                    break
   120→                cursor += 1
   121→
   122→            if chain_all_empty and cursor > chain_start + 1:
   123→                smell_counts["empty_if_chain"].append(
   124→                    {
   125→                        "file": filepath,
   126→                        "line": chain_start + 1,
   127→                        "content": lines[chain_start].strip()[:100],
   128→                    }
   129→                )
   130→            index = max(index + 1, cursor)
   131→            continue
   132→
   133→        index += 1
   134→
   135→
   136→def detect_dead_useeffects(
   137→    filepath: str,
   138→    lines: list[str],
   139→    smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]],
   140→    *,
   141→    scan_code_fn,
   142→    strip_ts_comments_fn,
   143→) -> None:
   144→    """Find useEffect calls with empty or comment-only bodies."""
   145→    for index, line in enumerate(lines):
   146→        stripped = line.strip()
   147→        if not re.match(r"(?:React\.)?useEffect\s*\(\s*\(\s*\)\s*=>\s*\{", stripped):
   148→            continue
   149→
   150→        paren_depth = 0
   151→        end = None
   152→        for cursor in range(index, min(index + 30, len(lines))):
   153→            for _, ch, in_string in scan_code_fn(lines[cursor]):
   154→                if in_string:
   155→                    continue
   156→                if ch == "(":
   157→                    paren_depth += 1
   158→                elif ch == ")":
   159→                    paren_depth -= 1
   160→                    if paren_depth <= 0:
   161→                        end = cursor
   162→                        break
   163→            if end is not None:
   164→                break
   165→
   166→        if end is None:
   167→            continue
   168→
   169→        text = "\n".join(lines[index : end + 1])
   170→        arrow_pos = text.find("=>")
   171→        if arrow_pos == -1:
   172→            continue
   173→        brace_pos = text.find("{", arrow_pos)
   174→        if brace_pos == -1:
   175→            continue
   176→
   177→        depth = 0
   178→        body_end = None
   179→        for cursor, ch, in_string in scan_code_fn(text, brace_pos):
   180→            if in_string:
   181→                continue
   182→            if ch == "{":
   183→                depth += 1
   184→            elif ch == "}":
   185→                depth -= 1
   186→                if depth == 0:
   187→                    body_end = cursor
   188→                    break
   189→
   190→        if body_end is None:
   191→            continue
   192→
   193→        body = text[brace_pos + 1 : body_end]
   194→        if strip_ts_comments_fn(body).strip() == "":
   195→            smell_counts["dead_useeffect"].append(
   196→                {
   197→                    "file": filepath,
   198→                    "line": index + 1,
   199→                    "content": stripped[:100],
   200→                }
   201→            )
   202→
   203→
   204→def detect_swallowed_errors(
   205→    filepath: str,
   206→    content: str,
   207→    lines: list[str],
   208→    smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]],
   209→    *,
   210→    scan_code_fn,
   211→    strip_ts_comments_fn,
   212→) -> None:
   213→    """Find catch blocks whose only content is console.error/warn/log."""
   214→    catch_re = re.compile(r"catch\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{")
   215→    for match in catch_re.finditer(content):
   216→        brace_start = match.end() - 1
   217→        depth = 0
   218→        body_end = None
   219→        for cursor, ch, in_string in scan_code_fn(
   220→            content,
   221→            brace_start,
   222→            min(brace_start + 500, len(content)),
   223→        ):
   224→            if in_string:
   225→                continue
   226→            if ch == "{":
   227→                depth += 1
   228→            elif ch == "}":
   229→                depth -= 1
   230→                if depth == 0:
   231→                    body_end = cursor
   232→                    break
   233→
   234→        if body_end is None:
   235→            continue
   236→
   237→        body = content[brace_start + 1 : body_end]
   238→        body_clean = strip_ts_comments_fn(body).strip()
   239→        if not body_clean:
   240→            continue
   241→
   242→        statements = [
   243→            stmt.strip().rstrip(";")
   244→            for stmt in re.split(r"[;\n]", body_clean)
   245→            if stmt.strip()
   246→        ]
   247→        if not statements:
   248→            continue
   249→
   250→        all_console = all(
   251→            re.match(r"console\.(error|warn|log)\s*\(", stmt) for stmt in statements
   252→        )
   253→        if all_console:
   254→            line_no = content[: match.start()].count("\n") + 1
   255→            smell_counts["swallowed_error"].append(
   256→                {
   257→                    "file": filepath,
   258→                    "line": line_no,
   259→                    "content": lines[line_no - 1].strip()[:100]
   260→                    if line_no <= len(lines)
   261→                    else "",
   262→                }
   263→            )
   264→
   265→
   266→def track_brace_body(
   267→    lines: list[str],
   268→    start_line: int,
   269→    *,
   270→    scan_code_fn,
   271→    max_scan: int = 2000,
   272→) -> int | None:
   273→    """Find closing brace that matches first opening brace from start_line."""
   274→    depth = 0
   275→    found_open = False
   276→    for line_idx in range(start_line, min(start_line + max_scan, len(lines))):
   277→        for _, ch, in_string in scan_code_fn(lines[line_idx]):
   278→            if in_string:
   279→                continue
   280→            if ch == "{":
   281→                depth += 1
   282→                found_open = True
   283→            elif ch == "}":
   284→                depth -= 1
   285→                if found_open and depth == 0:
   286→                    return line_idx
   287→    return None
   288→
   289→
   290→__all__ = [
   291→    "detect_dead_useeffects",
   292→    "detect_empty_if_chains",
   293→    "detect_error_no_throw",
   294→    "detect_swallowed_errors",
   295→    "track_brace_body",
   296→]
   297→
```

1→"""TypeScript function-level smell detectors — monster functions, dead functions, etc."""
     2→
     3→import re
     4→
     5→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_helpers import (
     6→    _strip_ts_comments,
     7→    _track_brace_body,
     8→    _ts_match_is_in_string,
     9→    scan_code,
    10→)
    11→
    12→_MAX_CATCH_BODY = 1000  # max characters to scan for catch block body
    13→_MAX_SWITCH_BODY_SCAN = 5000
    14→
    15→
    16→def _find_block_end(content: str, brace_start: int, max_scan: int) -> int | None:
    17→    depth = 0
    18→    for ci, ch, in_s in scan_code(content, brace_start, min(brace_start + max_scan, len(content))):
    19→        if in_s:
    20→            continue
    21→        if ch == "{":
    22→            depth += 1
    23→        elif ch == "}":
    24→            depth -= 1
    25→            if depth == 0:
    26→                return ci
    27→    return None
    28→
    29→
    30→def _find_function_start(line: str, next_lines: list[str]) -> str | None:
    31→    """Return function name for declarations/assignments, else None."""
    32→    stripped = line.strip()
    33→    if not stripped:
    34→        return None
    35→    if stripped.startswith(("interface ", "type ", "enum ", "class ")):
    36→        return None
    37→
    38→    declaration_match = re.match(
    39→        r"^(?:export\s+)?(?:default\s+)?(?:async\s+)?function\s+([A-Za-z_]\w*)\s*\(",
    40→        stripped,
    41→    )
    42→    if declaration_match:
    43→        return declaration_match.group(1)
    44→
    45→    assignment_match = re.match(
    46→        r"^(?:export\s+)?(?:const|let|var)\s+([A-Za-z_$][\w$]*)\b",
    47→        stripped,
    48→    )
    49→    if not assignment_match:
    50→        return None
    51→
    52→    combined = "\n".join(
    53→        [stripped] + [next_line.strip() for next_line in next_lines[:2]]
    54→    )
    55→    eq_pos = combined.find("=", assignment_match.end())
    56→    if eq_pos == -1:
    57→        return None
    58→    after_eq = combined[eq_pos + 1 :].lstrip()
    59→    if re.match(r"(?:async\s+)?\([^)]*\)\s*=>", after_eq):
    60→        return assignment_match.group(1)
    61→    if re.match(r"function\b", after_eq):
    62→        return assignment_match.group(1)
    63→    return None
    64→
    65→def _find_opening_brace_line(lines: list[str], start: int, *, window: int = 5) -> int | None:
    66→    for idx in range(start, min(start + window, len(lines))):
    67→        if "{" in lines[idx]:
    68→            return idx
    69→    return None
    70→
    71→
    72→def _detect_monster_functions(
    73→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
    74→):
    75→    """Find functions/components exceeding 150 LOC via brace-tracking.
    76→
    77→    Matches: function declarations, named arrow functions, and React components.
    78→    Skips: interfaces, types, enums, and objects/arrays.
    79→    """
    80→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
    81→        name = _find_function_start(line, lines[i + 1 : i + 3])
    82→        if not name:
    83→            continue
    84→
    85→        brace_line = _find_opening_brace_line(lines, i, window=5)
    86→        if brace_line is None:
    87→            continue
    88→
    89→        end_line = _track_brace_body(lines, brace_line, max_scan=2000)
    90→        if end_line is not None:
    91→            loc = end_line - i + 1
    92→            if loc > 150:
    93→                smell_counts["monster_function"].append(
    94→                    {
    95→                        "file": filepath,
    96→                        "line": i + 1,
    97→                        "content": f"{name}() — {loc} LOC",
    98→                    }
    99→                )
   100→
   101→
   102→def _detect_stub_functions(
   103→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   104→):
   105→    """Find functions with empty body or only return/return null (stub functions).
   106→
   107→    Matches function declarations and named arrow functions.
   108→    Skips decorated functions (TS decorators on line above).
   109→    """
   110→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   111→        # Skip decorated functions
   112→        if i > 0 and lines[i - 1].strip().startswith("@"):
   113→            continue
   114→
   115→        name = _find_function_start(line, lines[i + 1 : i + 3])
   116→        if not name:
   117→            continue
   118→
   119→        # Find opening brace
   120→        brace_line = None
   121→        for k in range(i, min(i + 5, len(lines))):
   122→            if "{" in lines[k]:
   123→                brace_line = k
   124→                break
   125→        if brace_line is None:
   126→            continue
   127→
   128→        end_line = _track_brace_body(lines, brace_line, max_scan=30)
   129→        if end_line is None:
   130→            continue
   131→
   132→        # Extract body between braces
   133→        body_text = "\n".join(lines[brace_line : end_line + 1])
   134→        first_brace = body_text.find("{")
   135→        last_brace = body_text.rfind("}")
   136→        if first_brace == -1 or last_brace == -1 or first_brace >= last_brace:
   137→            continue
   138→
   139→        body = body_text[first_brace + 1 : last_brace]
   140→        body_clean = _strip_ts_comments(body).strip().rstrip(";")
   141→
   142→        if body_clean in ("", "return", "return null", "return undefined"):
   143→            label = body_clean or "empty"
   144→            smell_counts["stub_function"].append(
   145→                {
   146→                    "file": filepath,
   147→                    "line": i + 1,
   148→                    "content": f"{name}() — body is {label}",
   149→                }
   150→            )
   151→
   152→
   153→def _detect_window_globals(
   154→    filepath: str,
   155→    lines: list[str],
   156→    line_state: dict[int, str],
   157→    smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]],
   158→):
   159→    """Find window.__* assignments — global state escape hatches.
   160→
   161→    Matches:
   162→    - window.__foo = ...
   163→    - (window as any).__foo = ...
   164→    - window['__foo'] = ...
   165→    """
   166→    window_re = re.compile(
   167→        r"""(?:"""
   168→        r"""\(?\s*window\s+as\s+any\s*\)?\s*\.\s*(__\w+)"""  # (window as any).__name
   169→        r"""|window\s*\.\s*(__\w+)"""  # window.__name
   170→        r"""|window\s*\[\s*['"](__\w+)['"]\s*\]"""  # window['__name']
   171→        r""")\s*=""",
   172→    )
   173→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   174→        if i in line_state:
   175→            continue
   176→        m = window_re.search(line)
   177→        if not m:
   178→            continue
   179→        if _ts_match_is_in_string(line, m.start()):
   180→            continue
   181→        smell_counts["window_global"].append(
   182→            {
   183→                "file": filepath,
   184→                "line": i + 1,
   185→                "content": line.strip()[:100],
   186→            }
   187→        )
   188→
   189→
   190→def _detect_catch_return_default(
   191→    filepath: str, content: str, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   192→):
   193→    """Find catch blocks that return object literals with default/no-op values.
   194→
   195→    Catches the pattern:
   196→      catch (...) { ... return { key: false, key: null, key: () => {} }; }
   197→
   198→    This is a silent failure — the caller gets valid-looking data but the
   199→    operation actually failed.
   200→    """
   201→    catch_re = re.compile(r"catch\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{")
   202→    for m in catch_re.finditer(content):
   203→        brace_start = m.end() - 1
   204→        depth = 0
   205→        body_end = None
   206→        for ci, ch, in_s in scan_code(
   207→            content, brace_start, min(brace_start + _MAX_CATCH_BODY, len(content))
   208→        ):
   209→            if in_s:
   210→                continue
   211→            if ch == "{":
   212→                depth += 1
   213→            elif ch == "}":
   214→                depth -= 1
   215→                if depth == 0:
   216→                    body_end = ci
   217→                    break
   218→
   219→        if body_end is None:
   220→            continue
   221→
   222→        body = content[brace_start + 1 : body_end]
   223→        # Check if body contains "return {" — a return with object literal
   224→        return_obj = re.search(r"\breturn\s*\{", body)
   225→        if not return_obj:
   226→            continue
   227→
   228→        # Extract the returned object content
   229→        obj_start = body.find("{", return_obj.start())
   230→        obj_depth = 0
   231→        obj_end = None
   232→        for ci, ch, in_s in scan_code(body, obj_start):
   233→            if in_s:
   234→                continue
   235→            if ch == "{":
   236→                obj_depth += 1
   237→            elif ch == "}":
   238→                obj_depth -= 1
   239→                if obj_depth == 0:
   240→                    obj_end = ci
   241→                    break
   242→
   243→        if obj_end is None:
   244→            continue
   245→
   246→        obj_content = body[obj_start + 1 : obj_end]
   247→        # Count default/no-op fields
   248→        noop_count = len(re.findall(r"\(\)\s*=>\s*\{\s*\}", obj_content))  # () => {}
   249→        false_count = len(
   250→            re.findall(r":\s*(?:false|null|undefined|0|''|\"\")\b", obj_content)
   251→        )
   252→        default_fields = noop_count + false_count
   253→
   254→        if default_fields >= 2:
   255→            line_no = content[: m.start()].count("\n") + 1
   256→            # Use content slice to get the line without re-splitting entire file
   257→            line_start = content.rfind("\n", 0, m.start()) + 1
   258→            line_end = content.find("\n", m.start())
   259→            if line_end == -1:
   260→                line_end = len(content)
   261→            smell_counts["catch_return_default"].append(
   262→                {
   263→                    "file": filepath,
   264→                    "line": line_no,
   265→                    "content": content[line_start:line_end].strip()[:100],
   266→                }
   267→            )
   268→
   269→
   270→def _extract_function_body(
   271→    lines: list[str], start_line: int,
   272→) -> str | None:
   273→    """Extract the inner body text of a function starting at start_line.
   274→
   275→    Returns the text between the opening and closing braces, or None.
   276→    """
   277→    brace_line = _find_opening_brace_line(lines, start_line, window=5)
   278→    if brace_line is None:
   279→        return None
   280→    end_line = _track_brace_body(lines, brace_line, max_scan=2000)
   281→    if end_line is None:
   282→        return None
   283→    body_text = "\n".join(lines[brace_line : end_line + 1])
   284→    first_brace = body_text.find("{")
   285→    last_brace = body_text.rfind("}")
   286→    if first_brace == -1 or last_brace == -1 or first_brace >= last_brace:
   287→        return None
   288→    return body_text[first_brace + 1 : last_brace]
   289→
   290→
   291→def _count_pattern_in_body(body: str, pattern: re.Pattern[str]) -> int:
   292→    """Count regex matches in body that aren't inside string literals."""
   293→    count = 0
   294→    for m in pattern.finditer(body):
   295→        pos = m.start()
   296→        line_start = body.rfind("\n", 0, pos) + 1
   297→        nl_pos = body.find("\n", pos)
   298→        line_text = body[line_start : nl_pos if nl_pos != -1 else len(body)]
   299→        if not _ts_match_is_in_string(line_text, pos - line_start):
   300→            count += 1
   301→    return count
   302→
   303→
   304→_FUNC_RE = re.compile(r"\bfunction\s*[\w(]")
   305→_ARROW_RE = re.compile(r"=>\s*\{")
   306→
   307→
   308→def _detect_nested_closures(
   309→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   310→):
   311→    """Find functions with >= 3 nested closure definitions."""
   312→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   313→        name = _find_function_start(line, lines[i + 1 : i + 3])
   314→        if not name:
   315→            continue
   316→
   317→        body = _extract_function_body(lines, i)
   318→        if body is None:
   319→            continue
   320→
   321→        closure_count = (
   322→            _count_pattern_in_body(body, _FUNC_RE)
   323→            + _count_pattern_in_body(body, _ARROW_RE)
   324→        )
   325→        if closure_count >= 3:
   326→            smell_counts["nested_closure"].append(
   327→                {
   328→                    "file": filepath,
   329→                    "line": i + 1,
   330→                    "content": f"{name}() — {closure_count} nested closures",
   331→                }
   332→            )
   333→
   334→
   335→_TS_BRANCH_PATTERNS = (
   336→    r"\bif\s*\(",
   337→    r"\belse\s+if\s*\(",
   338→    r"\bcase\s+",
   339→    r"\bcatch\s*\(",
   340→    r"\bfor\s*\(",
   341→    r"\bwhile\s*\(",
   342→)
   343→
   344→
   345→def _compute_ts_cyclomatic_complexity(body: str) -> int:
   346→    """Compute cyclomatic complexity for a TypeScript function body string."""
   347→    stripped = _strip_ts_comments(body)
   348→    complexity = 1
   349→    for pattern in _TS_BRANCH_PATTERNS:
   350→        complexity += len(re.findall(pattern, stripped))
   351→    complexity += len(re.findall(r"&&", stripped))
   352→    complexity += len(re.findall(r"\|\|", stripped))
   353→    complexity += len(re.findall(r"\?(?!=)", stripped))
   354→    return complexity
   355→
   356→
   357→def _detect_high_cyclomatic_complexity(
   358→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   359→):
   360→    """Flag functions with cyclomatic complexity > 15."""
   361→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   362→        name = _find_function_start(line, lines[i + 1 : i + 3])
   363→        if not name:
   364→            continue
   365→
   366→        body = _extract_function_body(lines, i)
   367→        if body is None:
   368→            continue
   369→
   370→        complexity = _compute_ts_cyclomatic_complexity(body)
   371→        if complexity > 15:
   372→            smell_counts["high_cyclomatic_complexity"].append(
   373→                {
   374→                    "file": filepath,
   375→                    "line": i + 1,
   376→                    "content": f"{name}() — cyclomatic complexity {complexity}",
   377→                }
   378→            )
   379→
   380→
   381→def _detect_switch_no_default(
   382→    filepath: str, content: str, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   383→):
   384→    """Flag switch statements that have no default case."""
   385→    switch_re = re.compile(r"\bswitch\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{")
   386→    for m in switch_re.finditer(content):
   387→        brace_start = m.end() - 1
   388→        depth = 0
   389→        body_end = None
   390→        for ci, ch, in_s in scan_code(
   391→            content,
   392→            brace_start,
   393→            min(brace_start + _MAX_SWITCH_BODY_SCAN, len(content)),
   394→        ):
   395→            if in_s:
   396→                continue
   397→            if ch == "{":
   398→                depth += 1
   399→            elif ch == "}":
   400→                depth -= 1
   401→                if depth == 0:
   402→                    body_end = ci
   403→                    break
   404→
   405→        if body_end is None:
   406→            continue
   407→
   408→        body = content[brace_start + 1 : body_end]
   409→        # Count case labels — only flag if there are actual cases
   410→        case_count = len(re.findall(r"\bcase\s+", body))
   411→        if case_count < 2:
   412→            continue
   413→
   414→        if re.search(r"\bdefault\s*:", body):
   415→            continue
   416→
   417→        line_no = content[: m.start()].count("\n") + 1
   418→        line_start = content.rfind("\n", 0, m.start()) + 1
   419→        line_end = content.find("\n", m.start())
   420→        if line_end == -1:
   421→            line_end = len(content)
   422→        smell_counts["switch_no_default"].append(
   423→            {
   424→                "file": filepath,
   425→                "line": line_no,
   426→                "content": content[line_start:line_end].strip()[:100],
   427→            }
   428→        )
   429→
```

   220→
   221→def _build_ts_line_state(lines: list[str]) -> dict[int, str]:
   222→    """Build a map of line numbers that are inside block comments or template literals.
   223→
   224→    Returns {0-indexed line: reason} where reason is "block_comment" or "template_literal".
   225→    Lines not in the map are normal code lines suitable for regex checks.
   226→
   227→    Tracks:
   228→    - Block comment state (opened by /*, closed by */)
   229→    - Template literal state (opened by backtick, closed by backtick,
   230→      with ${} nesting awareness)
   231→    """
   232→    state: dict[int, str] = {}
   233→    in_block_comment = False
   234→    in_template = False
   235→    template_brace_depth = 0  # tracks ${} nesting inside template literals
   236→
   237→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   238→        if in_block_comment:
   239→            state[i] = "block_comment"
   240→            if "*/" in line:
   241→                in_block_comment = False
   242→            continue
   243→
   244→        if in_template:
   245→            state[i] = "template_literal"
   246→            # Scan for closing backtick or ${} nesting
   247→            j = 0
   248→            while j < len(line):
   249→                ch = line[j]
   250→                if ch == "\\" and j + 1 < len(line):
   251→                    j += 2
   252→                    continue
   253→                if ch == "$" and j + 1 < len(line) and line[j + 1] == "{":
   254→                    template_brace_depth += 1
   255→                    j += 2
   256→                    continue
   257→                if ch == "}" and template_brace_depth > 0:
   258→                    template_brace_depth -= 1
   259→                    j += 1
   260→                    continue
   261→                if ch == "`" and template_brace_depth == 0:
   262→                    in_template = False
   263→                    # Rest of line is normal code — don't mark it
   264→                    # but we already marked the line; that's fine for
   265→                    # line-level filtering
   266→                    break
   267→                j += 1
   268→            continue
   269→
   270→        # Normal code line — check for block comment or template literal start
   271→        j = 0
   272→        in_str = None
   273→        while j < len(line):
   274→            ch = line[j]
   275→
   276→            # Skip escape sequences
   277→            if in_str and ch == "\\" and j + 1 < len(line):
   278→                j += 2
   279→                continue
   280→
   281→            # String tracking
   282→            if in_str:
   283→                if ch == in_str:
   284→                    in_str = None
   285→                j += 1
   286→                continue
   287→
   288→            # Line comment — rest is not code
   289→            if ch == "/" and j + 1 < len(line) and line[j + 1] == "/":
   290→                break
   291→
   292→            # Block comment start
   293→            if ch == "/" and j + 1 < len(line) and line[j + 1] == "*":
   294→                # Check if it closes on same line
   295→                close = line.find("*/", j + 2)
   296→                if close != -1:
   297→                    j = close + 2
   298→                    continue
   299→                else:
   300→                    in_block_comment = True
   301→                    break
   302→
   303→            # Template literal start
   304→            if ch == "`":
   305→                # Scan for closing backtick on same line
   306→                k = j + 1
   307→                found_close = False
   308→                depth = 0
   309→                while k < len(line):
   310→                    c = line[k]
   311→                    if c == "\\" and k + 1 < len(line):
   312→                        k += 2
   313→                        continue
   314→                    if c == "$" and k + 1 < len(line) and line[k + 1] == "{":
   315→                        depth += 1
   316→                        k += 2
   317→                        continue
   318→                    if c == "}" and depth > 0:
   319→                        depth -= 1
   320→                        k += 1
   321→                        continue
   322→                    if c == "`" and depth == 0:
   323→                        found_close = True
   324→                        j = k + 1
   325→                        break
   326→                    k += 1
   327→                if found_close:
   328→                    continue
   329→                else:
   330→                    in_template = True
   331→                    template_brace_depth = depth
   332→                    break
   333→
   334→            if ch in ("'", '"'):
   335→                in_str = ch
   336→                j += 1
   337→                continue
   338→
   339→            j += 1
   340→
   341→    return state
   342→
   343→
   344→def _detect_empty_if_chains(filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> None:
   345→    """Find if/else chains where all branches are empty."""
   346→    index = 0
   347→    while index < len(lines):
   348→        stripped = lines[index].strip()
   349→        if not re.match(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\(", stripped):
   350→            index += 1
   351→            continue
   352→
   353→        if re.match(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*\}\s*$", stripped):
   354→            chain_start = index
   355→            cursor = index + 1
   356→            while cursor < len(lines):
   357→                next_stripped = lines[cursor].strip()
   358→                if re.match(r"else\s+if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*\}\s*$", next_stripped):
   359→                    cursor += 1
   360→                    continue
   361→                if re.match(r"(?:\}\s*)?else\s*\{\s*\}\s*$", next_stripped):
   362→                    cursor += 1
   363→                    continue
   364→                break
   365→            smell_counts["empty_if_chain"].append(
   366→                {"file": filepath, "line": chain_start + 1, "content": stripped[:100]}
   367→            )
   368→            index = cursor
   369→            continue
   370→
   371→        if re.match(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*$", stripped):
   372→            chain_start = index
   373→            chain_all_empty = True
   374→            cursor = index
   375→            while cursor < len(lines):
   376→                current = lines[cursor].strip()
   377→                if cursor == chain_start:
   378→                    if not re.match(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*$", current):
   379→                        chain_all_empty = False
   380→                        break
   381→                elif re.match(r"\}\s*else\s+if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*$", current):
   382→                    pass
   383→                elif re.match(r"\}\s*else\s*\{\s*$", current):
   384→                    pass
   385→                elif current == "}":
   386→                    tail = cursor + 1
   387→                    while tail < len(lines) and lines[tail].strip() == "":
   388→                        tail += 1
   389→                    if tail < len(lines) and re.match(r"else\s", lines[tail].strip()):
   390→                        cursor = tail
   391→                        continue
   392→                    cursor += 1
   393→                    break
   394→                elif current == "":
   395→                    cursor += 1
   396→                    continue
   397→                else:
   398→                    chain_all_empty = False
   399→                    break
   400→                cursor += 1
   401→            if chain_all_empty and cursor > chain_start + 1:
   402→                smell_counts["empty_if_chain"].append(
   403→                    {"file": filepath, "line": chain_start + 1, "content": lines[chain_start].strip()[:100]}
   404→                )
   405→            index = max(index + 1, cursor)
   406→            continue
   407→
   408→        index += 1
   409→
   410→
   411→def _detect_dead_useeffects(filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> None:
   412→    """Find useEffect calls with empty/whitespace/comment-only bodies."""
   413→    for line_no, line in enumerate(lines):
   414→        stripped = line.strip()
   415→        if not re.match(r"(?:React\.)?useEffect\s*\(\s*\(\s*\)\s*=>\s*\{", stripped):
   416→            continue
   417→
   418→        paren_depth = 0
   419→        end_line = None
   420→        for cursor in range(line_no, min(line_no + 30, len(lines))):
   421→            for _, ch, in_string in scan_code(lines[cursor]):
   422→                if in_string:
   423→                    continue
   424→                if ch == "(":
   425→                    paren_depth += 1
   426→                elif ch == ")":
   427→                    paren_depth -= 1
   428→                    if paren_depth <= 0:
   429→                        end_line = cursor
   430→                        break
   431→            if end_line is not None:
   432→                break
   433→
   434→        if end_line is None:
   435→            continue
   436→
   437→        text = "\n".join(lines[line_no:end_line + 1])
   438→        arrow_pos = text.find("=>")
   439→        if arrow_pos == -1:
   440→            continue
   441→        brace_pos = text.find("{", arrow_pos)
   442→        if brace_pos == -1:
   443→            continue
   444→
   445→        depth = 0
   446→        body_end = None
   447→        for idx, ch, in_string in scan_code(text, brace_pos):
   448→            if in_string:
   449→                continue
   450→            if ch == "{":
   451→                depth += 1
   452→            elif ch == "}":
   453→                depth -= 1
   454→                if depth == 0:
   455→                    body_end = idx
   456→                    break
   457→        if body_end is None:
   458→            continue
   459→
   460→        body = text[brace_pos + 1:body_end]
   461→        if _strip_ts_comments(body).strip() == "":
   462→            smell_counts["dead_useeffect"].append(
   463→                {"file": filepath, "line": line_no + 1, "content": stripped[:100]}
   464→            )
   465→
   466→
   467→def _script_is_documented(readme_text: str, script_name: str) -> bool:
   468→    escaped = re.escape(script_name)
   469→    command_patterns = [
   470→        rf"\bnpm\s+(?:run\s+)?{escaped}\b",
   471→        rf"\bpnpm\s+{escaped}\b",
   472→        rf"\byarn\s+{escaped}\b",
   473→        rf"\bbun\s+run\s+{escaped}\b",
   474→    ]
   475→    if any(re.search(pattern, readme_text, flags=re.IGNORECASE) for pattern in command_patterns):
   476→        return True
   477→    return bool(re.search(rf"`{escaped}`", readme_text))
   478→
   479→
   480→def _detect_non_ts_asset_smells(path: Path, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> int:
   481→    """Scan adjacent non-TS assets (CSS/docs) for common repo-health smells."""
   482→    scanned_files = 0
   483→    css_files = find_source_files(path, [".css", ".scss", ".sass", ".less"])
   484→    scanned_files += len(css_files)
   485→
   486→    for filepath in css_files:
   487→        try:
   488→            full = Path(filepath) if Path(filepath).is_absolute() else PROJECT_ROOT / filepath
   489→            content = full.read_text()
   490→            lines = content.splitlines()
   491→        except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
   492→            log_best_effort_failure(logger, f"read stylesheet smell candidate {filepath}", exc)
   493→            continue
   494→
   495→        if len(lines) >= 300:
   496→            smell_counts["css_monolith"].append(
   497→                {
   498→                    "file": filepath,
   499→                    "line": 1,
   500→                    "content": f"{len(lines)} LOC stylesheet",
   501→                }
   502→            )
   503→
   504→        important_count = content.count("!important")
   505→        if important_count >= 8:
   506→            first_line = next(
   507→                (idx + 1 for idx, line in enumerate(lines) if "!important" in line),
   508→                1,
   509→            )
   510→            smell_counts["css_important_overuse"].append(
   511→                {
   512→                    "file": filepath,
   513→                    "line": first_line,
   514→                    "content": f"{important_count} !important declarations",
   515→                }
   516→            )
   517→
   518→    readme_path = PROJECT_ROOT / "README.md"
   519→    package_path = PROJECT_ROOT / "package.json"
   520→    if not readme_path.is_file() or not package_path.is_file():
   521→        return scanned_files
   522→
   523→    scanned_files += 1
   524→    try:
   525→        readme_text = readme_path.read_text()
   526→        package_payload = json.loads(package_path.read_text())
   527→    except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
   528→        log_best_effort_failure(logger, "read package/readme for docs drift smell", exc)
   529→        return scanned_files
   530→
   531→    scripts = package_payload.get("scripts")
   532→    if not isinstance(scripts, dict):
   533→        return scanned_files
   534→
   535→    key_scripts = [
   536→        script for script in ("dev", "build", "test", "lint", "typecheck") if script in scripts
   537→    ]
   538→    if len(key_scripts) < 2:
   539→        return scanned_files
   540→
   541→    missing = [script for script in key_scripts if not _script_is_documented(readme_text, script)]
   542→    if len(missing) >= 2:
   543→        smell_counts["docs_scripts_drift"].append(
   544→            {
   545→                "file": "README.md",
   546→                "line": 1,
   547→                "content": f"Missing script docs: {', '.join(missing[:5])}",
   548→            }
   549→        )
   550→    return scanned_files
   551→
   552→
   553→def detect_smells(path: Path) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
   554→    """Detect TypeScript/React code smell patterns across the codebase.
   555→
   556→    Returns (entries, total_files_checked).
   557→    """
   558→    checks = TS_SMELL_CHECKS
   559→    smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]] = {s["id"]: [] for s in checks}
   560→    files = find_ts_files(path)
   561→
   562→    for filepath in files:
   563→        if "node_modules" in filepath or ".d.ts" in filepath:
   564→            continue
   565→        try:
   566→            p = (
   567→                Path(filepath)
   568→                if Path(filepath).is_absolute()
   569→                else PROJECT_ROOT / filepath
   570→            )
   571→            content = p.read_text()
   572→            lines = content.splitlines()
   573→        except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
   574→            log_best_effort_failure(
   575→                logger, f"read TypeScript smell candidate {filepath}", exc
   576→            )
   577→            continue
   578→
   579→        # Build line state for string/comment filtering
   580→        line_state = _build_ts_line_state(lines)
   581→
   582→        # Regex-based smells
   583→        for check in checks:
   584→            if check["pattern"] is None:
   585→                continue
   586→            for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   587→                # Skip lines inside block comments or template literals
   588→                if i in line_state:
   589→                    continue
   590→                m = re.search(check["pattern"], line)
   591→                if not m:
   592→                    continue
   593→                # Check if match is inside a single-line string or comment
   594→                if _ts_match_is_in_string(line, m.start()):
   595→                    continue
   596→                # Skip URLs assigned to module-level constants
   597→                if check["id"] == "hardcoded_url" and re.match(
   598→                    r"^(?:export\s+)?(?:const|let|var)\s+[A-Z_][A-Z0-9_]*\s*=",
   599→                    line.strip(),
   600→                ):
   601→                    continue
   602→                smell_counts[check["id"]].append(
   603→                    {
   604→                        "file": filepath,
   605→                        "line": i + 1,
   606→                        "content": line.strip()[:100],
   607→                    }
   608→                )
   609→
   610→        # Multi-line smell helpers (brace-tracked)
   611→        _detect_async_no_await(filepath, content, lines, smell_counts)
   612→        _detect_error_no_throw(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   613→        _detect_empty_if_chains(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   614→        _detect_dead_useeffects(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   615→        _detect_swallowed_errors(filepath, content, lines, smell_counts)
   616→        _detect_monster_functions(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   617→        _detect_stub_functions(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   618→        _detect_window_globals(filepath, lines, line_state, smell_counts)
   619→        _detect_catch_return_default(filepath, content, smell_counts)
   620→        _detect_switch_no_default(filepath, content, smell_counts)
   621→        _detect_nested_closures(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   622→        _detect_high_cyclomatic_complexity(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   623→
   624→    non_ts_files = _detect_non_ts_asset_smells(path, smell_counts)
   625→
   626→    # Build summary entries sorted by severity then count
   627→    severity_order = {"high": 0, "medium": 1, "low": 2}
   628→    entries = []
   629→    for check in checks:
   630→        matches = smell_counts[check["id"]]
   631→        if matches:
   632→            entries.append(
   633→                {
   634→                    "id": check["id"],
   635→                    "label": check["label"],
   636→                    "severity": check["severity"],
   637→                    "count": len(matches),
   638→                    "files": len(set(m["file"] for m in matches)),
   639→                    "matches": matches[:50],
   640→                }
   641→            )
   642→    entries.sort(key=lambda e: (severity_order.get(e["severity"], 9), -e["count"]))
   643→    return entries, len(files) + non_ts_files
   644→
```

1→"""String-aware TypeScript character scanner."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections.abc import Generator
     6→
     7→
     8→def scan_code(
     9→    text: str, start: int = 0, end: int | None = None
    10→) -> Generator[tuple[int, str, bool], None, None]:
    11→    """Yield ``(index, char, in_string)`` tuples while handling escapes."""
    12→    i = start
    13→    limit = end if end is not None else len(text)
    14→    in_str = None
    15→    while i < limit:
    16→        ch = text[i]
    17→        if in_str:
    18→            if ch == "\\" and i + 1 < limit:
    19→                yield (i, ch, True)
    20→                i += 1
    21→                yield (i, text[i], True)
    22→                i += 1
    23→                continue
    24→            if ch == in_str:
    25→                in_str = None
    26→            yield (i, ch, in_str is not None)
    27→        else:
    28→            if ch in ("'", '"', "`"):
    29→                in_str = ch
    30→                yield (i, ch, True)
    31→            else:
    32→                yield (i, ch, False)
    33→        i += 1
    34→
    35→

    71→    )
    72→    if inline_types > 3:
    73→        return inline_types, f"{inline_types} inline types"
    74→    return None

    22→def detect_state_sync(path: Path) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
    23→    """Find useEffect blocks whose only statements are setState calls.
    24→
    25→    This pattern causes an unnecessary extra render cycle — the derived value
    26→    is stale for one frame. Common variants:
    27→    - "Derived state": should be useMemo or inline computation
    28→    - "Reset on change": should use key prop or restructure
    29→
    30→    Returns one entry per occurrence with setter names and line number.
    31→    """
    32→    entries = []
    33→    total_effects = 0
    34→
    35→    for filepath in find_tsx_files(path):
    36→        try:
    37→            p = (
    38→                Path(filepath)
    39→                if Path(filepath).is_absolute()
    40→                else PROJECT_ROOT / filepath
    41→            )
    42→            content = p.read_text()
    43→            lines = content.splitlines()
    44→        except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
    45→            logger.debug(
    46→                "Skipping unreadable TSX file %s in state-sync pass: %s", filepath, exc
    47→            )
    48→            continue
    49→
    50→        # Collect all useState setters in this file

1→"""Small syntax helpers reused by TypeScript fixers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_helpers import scan_code
     6→
     7→_CHAR_DEPTH_DELTA: dict[str, tuple[str, int]] = {
     8→    "(": ("parens", 1),
     9→    ")": ("parens", -1),
    10→    "{": ("braces", 1),
    11→    "}": ("braces", -1),
    12→    "[": ("brackets", 1),
    13→    "]": ("brackets", -1),
    14→}
    15→
    16→
    17→def find_balanced_end(
    18→    lines: list[str], start: int, *, track: str = "parens", max_lines: int = 80
    19→) -> int | None:
    20→    """Find the line where brackets opened at *start* balance to zero."""
    21→    depths = {"parens": 0, "braces": 0, "brackets": 0}
    22→    for idx in range(start, min(start + max_lines, len(lines))):
    23→        for _, ch, in_s in scan_code(lines[idx]):
    24→            if in_s:
    25→                continue
    26→            delta_spec = _CHAR_DEPTH_DELTA.get(ch)
    27→            if delta_spec is None:
    28→                continue
    29→            key, delta = delta_spec
    30→            depths[key] += delta
    31→            if delta > 0:
    32→                continue
    33→            if track == "parens" and key == "parens" and depths["parens"] <= 0:
    34→                return idx
    35→            if track == "braces" and key == "braces" and depths["braces"] <= 0:
    36→                return idx
    37→            if track == "all" and key == "parens" and depths["parens"] <= 0:
    38→                return idx
    39→    return None
    40→
    41→
    42→def extract_body_between_braces(text: str, search_after: str = "") -> str | None:
    43→    """Extract content between the first ``{`` and its matching ``}``."""
    44→    start_pos = 0
    45→    if search_after:
    46→        pos = text.find(search_after)
    47→        if pos == -1:
    48→            return None
    49→        start_pos = pos + len(search_after)
    50→

1→"""Empty if-chain fixer: deletes if/else chains where all branches are empty."""
     2→
     3→from typing import Any
     4→
     5→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_helpers import scan_code

     9→
    10→def fix_empty_if_chain(
    11→    entries: list[dict[str, Any]],
    12→    *,
    13→    dry_run: bool = False,
    14→) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    15→    """Delete if/else chains where all branches are empty."""
    16→
    17→    def transform(
    18→        lines: list[str],
    19→        file_entries: list[dict[str, Any]],
    20→    ) -> tuple[list[str], list[str]]:
    21→        lines_to_remove: set[int] = set()
    22→
    23→        for e in file_entries:
    24→            line_idx = e["line"] - 1
    25→            if line_idx < 0 or line_idx >= len(lines):
    26→                continue
    27→
    28→            end = _find_if_chain_end(lines, line_idx)
    29→            for idx in range(line_idx, end + 1):
    30→                lines_to_remove.add(idx)
    31→
    32→        new_lines = collapse_blank_lines(lines, lines_to_remove)
    33→        return new_lines, ["empty_if_chain"]
    34→
    35→    return apply_fixer(entries, transform, dry_run=dry_run)
    36→
    37→
    38→def _find_if_chain_end(lines: list[str], start: int) -> int:
    39→    """Find the last line of an if/else chain starting at `start`.
    40→
    41→    Tracks brace depth. Chain ends when braces balance and no else follows.
    42→    """
    43→    brace_depth = 0
    44→    found_brace = False
    45→
    46→    for i in range(start, min(start + 100, len(lines))):
    47→        line = lines[i]
    48→        for ci, ch, in_s in scan_code(line):
    49→            if in_s:
    50→                continue

1→"""Text-oriented utility helpers split from the main utils facade."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import os
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→
     8→from desloppify.core.runtime_state import current_runtime_context
     9→
    10→_DEFAULT_PROJECT_ROOT = Path(os.environ.get("DESLOPPIFY_ROOT", Path.cwd())).resolve()
    11→
    12→# Legacy module-level constant — kept for backwards compat, but prefer
    13→# get_project_root() which respects RuntimeContext overrides.
    14→PROJECT_ROOT = _DEFAULT_PROJECT_ROOT
    15→
    16→
    17→def get_project_root() -> Path:
    18→    """Return the active project root, checking RuntimeContext first.
    19→
    20→    Tests set ``RuntimeContext.project_root`` to point at a tmp directory.
    21→    Production code uses the process-level default from $DESLOPPIFY_ROOT / cwd.
    22→    """
    23→    override = current_runtime_context().project_root
    24→    if override is not None:
    25→        return override
    26→    return _DEFAULT_PROJECT_ROOT
    27→
    28→
    29→def read_code_snippet(
    30→    filepath: str,
    31→    line: int,
    32→    context: int = 1,
    33→    *,
    34→    project_root: Path | str | None = None,
    35→) -> str | None:
    36→    """Read ±context lines around a line number. Returns formatted string or None."""
    37→    try:
    38→        root = (
    39→            Path(project_root).resolve()
    40→            if project_root is not None
    41→            # Runtime-aware default; preserves RuntimeContext/project-root overrides.
    42→            else get_project_root()
    43→        )
    44→        full = Path(filepath)
    45→        if not full.is_absolute():
    46→            full = root / full
    47→        content = full.read_text(errors="replace")
    48→    except OSError:
    49→        return None
    50→    lines = content.splitlines()
    51→    if line < 1 or line > len(lines):
    52→        return None
    53→    start = max(0, line - 1 - context)
    54→    end = min(len(lines), line + context)
    55→    parts = []
    56→    for i in range(start, end):
    57→        ln = i + 1
    58→        marker = "→" if ln == line else " "
    59→        text = lines[i]
    60→        if len(text) > 120:
    61→            text = text[:117] + "..."
    62→        parts.append(f"    {marker} {ln:>4} │ {text}")
    63→    return "\n".join(parts)
    64→
    65→
    66→def get_area(filepath: str, *, min_depth: int = 2) -> str:
    67→    """Derive an area name from a file path (generic: first 2 path components).
    68→
    69→    *min_depth* controls the minimum number of path components required before
    70→    the two-component area is returned.  The default (2) means a path like
    71→    ``"a/b"`` already qualifies.  Python uses ``min_depth=3`` so that
    72→    ``"pkg/mod.py"`` returns just ``"pkg"`` while ``"pkg/sub/mod.py"`` returns
    73→    ``"pkg/sub"``.
    74→    """
    75→    text = (filepath or "").strip()
    76→    if not text:
    77→        return "(unknown)"
    78→    parts = Path(text).parts
    79→    if not parts:
    80→        return "(unknown)"
    81→    return "/".join(parts[:2]) if len(parts) >= min_depth else parts[0]
    82→
    83→
    84→def strip_c_style_comments(text: str) -> str:
    85→    """Strip // and /* */ comments while preserving string literals."""
    86→    result: list[str] = []
    87→    i = 0
    88→    in_str = None
    89→    while i < len(text):
    90→        ch = text[i]
    91→        if in_str:
    92→            if ch == "\\" and i + 1 < len(text):
    93→                result.append(text[i : i + 2])
    94→                i += 2
    95→                continue
    96→            if ch == in_str:
    97→                in_str = None
    98→            result.append(ch)
    99→            i += 1
   100→        elif ch in ('"', "'", "`"):
   101→            in_str = ch
   102→            result.append(ch)
   103→            i += 1
   104→        elif ch == "/" and i + 1 < len(text):
   105→            if text[i + 1] == "/":
   106→                nl = text.find("\n", i)
   107→                if nl == -1:
   108→                    break
   109→                i = nl
   110→            elif text[i + 1] == "*":
   111→                end = text.find("*/", i + 2)
   112→                if end == -1:
   113→                    break
   114→                i = end + 2
   115→            else:
   116→                result.append(ch)
   117→                i += 1
   118→        else:
   119→            result.append(ch)
   120→            i += 1
   121→    return "".join(result)
   122→
   123→
   124→def is_numeric(value: object) -> bool:
   125→    """Return True if *value* is an int or float but NOT a bool.
   126→
   127→    Python's ``bool`` is a subclass of ``int``, so ``isinstance(True, int)``
   128→    is ``True``.  Many JSON-derived payloads need to distinguish real numbers
   129→    from booleans; this helper centralises that guard.
   130→    """
   131→    return isinstance(value, int | float) and not isinstance(value, bool)
   132→
   133→
   134→__all__ = [
   135→    "PROJECT_ROOT",
   136→    "get_area",
   137→    "get_project_root",
   138→    "is_numeric",
   139→    "read_code_snippet",
   140→    "strip_c_style_comments",
   141→]
   142→
