## Plan Verification and Detailed Implementation Order

### Verified Import Dependency Graph (source files only, excluding build/)

**`_smell_effects.py`** consumed by:
1. `_smell_helpers.py` -- imports all 5 public names (detect_dead_useeffects, detect_empty_if_chains, detect_error_no_throw, detect_swallowed_errors, track_brace_body)
2. `tests/commands/test_direct_coverage_modules.py` -- imports module as `ts_smell_effects`, references `ts_smell_effects.detect_swallowed_errors`

**`_smell_helpers.py`** consumed by:
1. `_smell_detectors.py` -- imports `_strip_ts_comments`, `_track_brace_body`, `_ts_match_is_in_string`, `scan_code`
2. `smells.py` -- imports `_detect_async_no_await`, `_detect_error_no_throw`, `_detect_swallowed_errors`, `_strip_ts_comments`, `_ts_match_is_in_string`, `scan_code`
3. `react.py` -- imports `_strip_ts_comments`, `scan_code`
4. `fixers/if_chain.py` -- imports `scan_code`
5. `fixers/syntax_scan.py` -- imports `scan_code`
6. `tests/smells/test_ts_smell_helpers.py` -- imports `_detect_async_no_await`, `_detect_dead_useeffects`, `_detect_empty_if_chains`, `_detect_error_no_throw`, `_detect_swallowed_errors`, `_strip_ts_comments`, `_track_brace_body`, `_ts_match_is_in_string`

**`_smell_detectors.py`** consumed by:
1. `smells.py` -- imports all 7 detector functions
2. `tests/smells/test_ts_smell_helpers.py` -- imports `_detect_catch_return_default`, `_detect_monster_functions`, `_detect_stub_functions`, `_detect_window_globals`, `_find_function_start`

### Issues Identified Beyond the Original Plan

**1. The `smells.py` local copies vs `_smell_effects.py` copies are functionally identical but use direct imports instead of DI.**

The `smells.py` versions of `_detect_empty_if_chains` and `_detect_dead_useeffects` call `scan_code` and `_strip_ts_comments` directly. The `_smell_effects.py` versions take them as keyword arguments (`scan_code_fn`, `strip_ts_comments_fn`). When merging into `_smell_detectors.py`, we should use the direct-call style (matching `smells.py`'s versions), which is what the plan proposes.

**2. `test_direct_coverage_modules.py` imports `_smell_effects` directly.**

Line 72: `import desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_effects as ts_smell_effects`
Line 178: `ts_smell_effects.detect_swallowed_errors`

This test is a smoke-test that just verifies modules are importable and functions are callable. After deleting `_smell_effects.py`, this test needs updating to reference `_smell_detectors` instead. **This was missing from the original plan's import update list.**

**3. `_track_brace_body` placement decision.**

The plan says `_track_brace_body` stays in `_smell_helpers.py`. This is correct because `_smell_detectors.py` imports it from `_smell_helpers.py`, and the tests import it from `_smell_helpers.py`. However, the current implementation in `_smell_helpers.py` delegates to `_smell_effects.py` via DI. After deleting `_smell_effects.py`, `_smell_helpers.py` needs to inline the `track_brace_body` implementation (currently 22 lines in `_smell_effects.py` lines 266-287). The plan mentions "inline implementation, remove DI" which is correct.

**4. `_build_ts_line_state` is only used inside `smells.py`.**

Moving it to `_smell_helpers.py` increases the public surface of `_smell_helpers.py` without a current consumer outside `smells.py`. It is still a reasonable move for separation of concerns (FSM utility vs orchestration), but it is optional. It does reduce `smells.py` by ~120 lines which is the stated goal.

**5. `build/` directory has stale copies.**

The `build/lib/` directory contains old copies of all four files. These should be cleaned up (or the build directory rebuilt) after the refactoring, but this is outside the scope of the code change itself.

**6. Choosing which version of the duplicated detectors to keep.**

For `_detect_empty_if_chains`: the `smells.py` version (lines 344-408) and `_smell_effects.py` version (lines 54-133) are functionally identical. The `_smell_effects.py` version does not take DI params for this function (unlike `detect_dead_useeffects`). They are the same code. Use either one.

For `_detect_dead_useeffects`: the `smells.py` version (lines 411-464) calls `scan_code` directly; the `_smell_effects.py` version (lines 136-201) takes `scan_code_fn` and `strip_ts_comments_fn` as keyword args. Use the `smells.py` version (direct calls) since we are eliminating DI.

### Risks and Mitigations

**Risk 1: Behavioral divergence between duplicate copies.**
I compared the `smells.py` and `_smell_effects.py` versions line by line. They are functionally identical (same regex patterns, same logic, same output format). The only differences are: (a) DI vs direct imports, and (b) minor whitespace/formatting. No behavioral risk.

**Risk 2: Tests currently exercise the `_smell_helpers` wrappers, which go through `_smell_effects` DI path.**
After the refactoring, the tests will import from `_smell_detectors` and call functions that use direct imports. If any test relied on the DI mechanism (e.g., mocking `scan_code_fn`), it would break. Checking `test_ts_smell_helpers.py`: no tests mock the DI parameters. All tests call the high-level wrapper functions. No risk.

**Risk 3: The `scan_code` re-export chain.**
Currently: `syntax/scanner.py` -> `_smell_helpers.py` -> `_smell_detectors.py`, `smells.py`, `react.py`, `fixers/`. After refactoring, `_smell_helpers.py` continues to re-export `scan_code`. `_smell_detectors.py` will need to import `scan_code` -- it can continue getting it from `_smell_helpers` (current behavior), or import directly from `syntax.scanner`. The plan should specify this. Recommendation: keep importing from `_smell_helpers` to avoid proliferating direct references to the scanner module. The `_smell_helpers` module serves as the canonical import point for `scan_code` within the detectors package.

**Risk 4: Circular imports.**
After the refactoring, `_smell_detectors.py` imports from `_smell_helpers.py` (utilities), and `smells.py` imports from both. No circular dependency. `_smell_helpers.py` imports only from `syntax.scanner` and `core.text_api`. Clean.

### Step-by-Step Implementation Order

The key principle: make each step independently testable. Run tests after each step.

**Step 1: Inline `_track_brace_body` in `_smell_helpers.py` (remove DI delegation)**

File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/typescript/detectors/_smell_helpers.py`

Replace lines 185-198 (the wrapper that delegates to `_track_brace_body_impl` with DI) with the direct implementation from `_smell_effects.py` lines 266-287, but importing `scan_code` directly instead of via parameter. Specifically:

```python
def _track_brace_body(
    lines: list[str], start_line: int, *, max_scan: int = 2000
) -> int | None:
    """Find the closing brace that matches the first opening brace from start_line."""
    depth = 0
    found_open = False
    for line_idx in range(start_line, min(start_line + max_scan, len(lines))):
        for _, ch, in_string in scan_code(lines[line_idx]):
            if in_string:
                continue
            if ch == "{":
                depth += 1
                found_open = True
            elif ch == "}":
                depth -= 1
                if found_open and depth == 0:
                    return line_idx
    return None
```

Remove the import of `track_brace_body as _track_brace_body_impl` from `_smell_effects`.
